# Global Strategy

# Quantamental Signal Ideas - Top & Bottom Ranked Stocks

Details on the methodology/model can be found in our Q-Series report

## What are the model’s recommendations today?

Based on the stock-level scores and an optimisation for portfolio balance, we find that the highest-scoring stocks tend to come from sectors including Transportation, Insurance, Communication Services and Cap Goods. The lowest-scoring stock list is dominated by Household Personal Products, Health Care, Food Beverage & Tobacco, Semis, and Staples stocks. Do note these recommendations can differ from the fundamental views of our equity strategy team.

## Digging into the details, what key message is each signal cohort suggesting?

• Price momentum has started to fade in Europe & Canada, and even turned negative again in China. Most favoured sectors remain Financials, Tech & Energy. Utilities now exhibits the worst momentum, after losing a lot of traction in the last 4/5 weeks.

• Not much change in positioning vs. last month. It remains very supportive in China, while being more balanced in other regions. In the US and Europe, Real estate is still the sector to overweight based on positioning, while in other regions, the top pick is Info Tech. Staples is the sector to stay away from at the moment, as investors are still building shorts, without any sign of crowding yet.

• Earnings expectations and momentum continue to favour Real Estate (ex China). Materials stocks seem to face some (strong) challenges recently, as EPS momentum has seriously weakened, turning negative this month. Defensive sectors are still struggling to move up in the ranking, despite being in the late stage of the business cycle.

• OECD G20 Composite Leading index' latest print and revision suggest we will soon switch from an expansion to a slowdown regime. This should be less bullish cyclicals and as a result, our regime model is getting prepared for a likely sector rotation. Energy has been cut first. Is Industrials next?

• On valuation, Financials & Utilities remain the cheapest sectors in our adjusted 12mth fwd PE model, when Tech & Health Care remain the most expensive. Value is a long term game. Since the last update, relative valuations have (unsurprisingly) worsened in Tech. • After Health Care in May, it is Staples which experienced a significant improvement in its Sector PMI new orders in June. Coming back to Health Care PMI, it continued its exponential rise, which our model wants to fade, given the high probability of a shortterm mean reversion.

## What are the industries whose rankings have changed the most?

Overall, the model is still displaying a risk-on stance but has reduced its level of conviction by 20% (the average signal is at +0.39 vs. +0.51 last month). Sector PMIs and OECD CLIs are both pointing to a less supportive macro environment. Energy, Materials Pharma Bio and Banks are the industries exhibiting the biggest drops in scoring.

UBS publishes a report that utilizes a quantamental framework for single stocks known as the "Quantamental Signals Strategy." The model uses 11 high-performing indicators to filter a trading signal for each stock globally. An easy-to-read dashboard that includes all stocks listed on the S&P 500, Eurostoxx 600, MSCI China, Nikkei 225, S&P/ASX 200 and S&P/TSX is available, and you should contact your UBS sales representative or the Quantitative Research team at qa@ubs.com for details and pricing.

## What our US (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/ lowest ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Consumer Discretionary

Robin Farley on Royal Caribbean Cruises: RCL is best positioned to benefit from continued strong cruise demand environment. We believe a key indication that cruise price has room to grow is that the gap to land-based hotel price in the US is still considerably wider than it was in ’19, even though U.S. hotel rate increases have been primarily driven by leisure demand coming out of the pandemic.

Jay Sole on Ralph Lauren Corp: We have a Buy rating on RL. We think the market doesn't appreciate the transformational changes RL has made to its brand image, distribution model, and cost structure. We forecast a 10% 5-yr. EPS CAGR.

Robin Farley on Marriott International: With a much more asset-light business than in prior macro cycles, MAR is fairly well positioned to benefit from recovery in business transit demand. For the industry, however, we have been tagging decline in occupancy which could prompt rate declines. In a more subdued RevPAR growth environment, unit growth becomes a more important driven of growth for c-corps. We prefer HLT over MAR given Hilton’s higher unit growth and better flow through of unit growth to the bottom line.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Staples

Faham Baig on Altria Group Inc: We have a Sell rating on Altria. The group is facing headwinds from a more challenged US market (100% of EBIT), where it is investing behind lower-priced Marlboro Black, and should result in Smokeable sales and OCI declining in FY24. The company is also attempting to catch up in next-generation products (NGP), which will prove difficult, in our view, resulting in Altria's share of the nicotine category declining. Whilst the recently announced buyback has resulted in the shares bouncing, we do not believe it is sustainable.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Energy

Manav Gupta on Kinder Morgan inc: Highest leverage in midstream space to trend of growing power demand from data centers. Utilization levels on KMI assets are approaching maximum today, so we expect the company to expand its current infrastructure and capture incremental growth in the US Southeast, Midcontinent, desert Southwest, and Texas.

Manav Gupta on Marathon Petroleum Corp: At present 100% of MPC’s dividend and 50% of MPC’s Capex is paid using the distribution from MPLX (\$2.2Bn). If MPLX grows its distribution by 10% for just 2 more years, 100% of MPC’s capex will also be fully covered by the distribution MPLX pays to MPC.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Financials

Timothy Chiodo on Paypal Holdings Inc: We are Neutral on the stock given gross profit growth challenges associated with both the core Branded Checkout button and the impacts associated with the migration of three legacy business (PayPal Pro, PayPal Here, and PayPal Invoices). While we expect continued gross profit growth pressures (albeit partially due to factors that will either partially lap or become less meaningful into 2024), uncertainty around operating expenses required (initiatives underway, expectation for a degree of ramping in 2H 2024), and increasing competitive pressures (existing concerns around Shop Pay, Apple Pay, etc., which are recently heightened by Apple Pay’s move to desktop outside iOS) to limit the attractiveness of the shares near-term. The path for PayPal shares to become more attractive would require additional confidence in a more meaningful (and durable) resumption in total company gross profit growth. For balance, we note near-term results (Q2 2024) are expected to be strong, given management’s recent comments around quarter-to-date trends.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Health Care

AJ Rice on HCA Healthcare Inc: We have a Buy rating on HCA. The company is the leading provider of healthcare services in the US, with its portfolio of hospitals concentrated in the fastest growing markets across the US. The company started 2024 with with strong patient volumes, with adjusted admissions growing 5.2% Y/Y. At recent conference presentations, management has suggested that volume trends remain strong, suggesting that the company could exceed its full year volume growth target of 3-4%. Cost trends and pricing are also tracking well, with comparisons for hospital based physician costs expected to ease in 2H24.

Kevin Caliendo on Cardinal Health Inc: We see the upcoming fiscal year (FY25) as a transition year as CAH folds in the benefit of Specialty Networks acquisition and customer wins, which serve to offset the effect of the Optum contract loss to McKesson. We believe these impacts are accounted for in guidance and consensus’ models but believe upside could be driven by better Rx market trends, ramp of nascent specialty offerings, growth of largest customers and upside from vaccine distribution. The Medical Segment remains a longer-term turnaround story; we believe shares can re-rate on the back of better Pharma segment growth and potential for upside if they can hit their targets in Medical.

Danielle Antalffy on Dexcom Inc: We continue to believe that DXCM is in the midst of another sales inflection as basal ramps in the U.S. and international sales growth accelerates through expanding access into more geographies reinforcing our belief in consistent 2024 sales upside. DXCM remains one of two major players to benefit from what we believe is CGM's move to standard of care across all 25M-30M+ diabetics in the U.S. within the next 5-7 years. With now multiple consecutive quarters of record new patient starts, we believe DXCM is well positioned to deliver sales growth acceleration from here and with it meaningful sales and margin upside.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Health Care

Kevin Caliendo on Henry Schein Inc: We are neutral on the stock. We remain on the sidelines as HSIC faces a challenging set up related to: (1) caution in spend for higher end dental equipment related to higher interest rates; (2) lower demand for higher spend dental procedures as patients become incrementally more economically sensitive and (3) lingering impacts from the cyber incident. On the positive side, dental visits appear healthy which read positively for HSIC’s consumables and equipment pricing deflation appears to be stabilizing.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Industrials

Thomas Wadewitz on CSX Corp: We have a Buy rating on the stock. We believe a trend of improving volume growth provides support for CSX while the transition to anticipated margin expansion in 2H24 against easier comparisons should also provide support for EPS performance and the stock.

Steven Fisher on Paccar Inc: We have a Buy rating on Paccar. We think there’s upside to consensus numbers on expectations for a truck upcycle in 2025-2026, even as we start a down year in 2024. We also think there are underappreciated company specific factors for PCAR that will drive margins ahead of consensus.

John Lovallo on Masco Corp: We are Buy rated on Masco. We continue to favor smaller ticket, highly branded building products like the BEHR paint and Delta plumbing components offered by MAS. We believe these products will hold up better than larger ticket, more discretionary items if the consumer softens in 2H.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Industrials

Damian Karas on Johnson Controls International: We recently downgraded the stock to Neutral, as we see a difficult path to FY24 guide and growth continuing to lag peers. Potential divestitures of non-core assets (25% of portfolio) represent modest upside to our PT (Link).

Kevin McVeigh on Paychex Inc: we are Neutral on the stock because 1) durable growth + FCF generation—constructive longer-term, near-term we believe the stock isn’t discounting enough macro concern, 2) stock has been option for yield investors given durable FCF, leading margins, amid \~3.2% dividend yield, 3) levered to SMBs, which could be challenging in late 2024-25 as it anniversaries down-market hiring which absorbed upmarket slack, and 4) \$120 PT PAYX is 24x 2026E EPS ex-cash—compared to 3-yr range of 23.2x-35.2x + average of 28.7x. Price Target based on 2 year multiple on CY26 estimates, rolled forward by Cost of Equity to derive 1 year Price Target.

Gavin Parsons on RTX Corp: We are Neutral on RTX. The company is well positioned in both Aerospace and Defense, but faces challenges within each. It is making progress with 1/3rd of GTF customer compensation agreements complete, but this means there is still risk that the \$3bn guided cash flow impact could evolve - positively or negatively - and believe a tangible update on that could still be a few quarters away.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in US Info Tech

Jon Windham on Solaredge Technologies Inc: We are Neutral on the stock. Residential solar continues to face headwinds from higher interest rates and excess inventory in the supply chain.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Materials

Curt Woodworth on Nucor Corp: We continue to view Nucor as leverage play on US infrastructure spending and see the stock as compelling on valuation based on mid-cycle analysis. US steel fundamentals are nearing an inflection point as destocking is fading and import levels are receding on the closed arbitrage.

## Highest-scoring stocks in US Utilities

William Appicelli on Constellation Energy: We see CEG as the best positioned name within Power having, post the IRA, secured the nuclear production tax credit which provides a floor price of \$43.75/MWh on generation output from the nuclear fleet. As the largest unregulated nuclear power company CEG is also the best positioned to sign nuclear offtake agreements with data center and large load commercial customers seeking around the clock, clean power.

William Appicelli on Eversource Energy: Biggest utility discount outside of California at a 20% discount 12x ’25 EPS of \$4.87. Exit of offshore wind will help derisk the company and improve the balance sheet. ES has also stated they are exploring the sale of CT water utility Aquarion. We estimate that the company can deliver at least 13%FFO/debt in 2025 and likely in the \~14% range which is consistent with ES' Baa2/A- ratings and stated 14-15% target.

## What our European (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/lowest-ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Communication Services

Adam Berlin on Universal Music Group: We have a Buy rating on UMG. We believe a combination of momentum in paid streaming (penetration and price rises), cost savings and limited further spend on catalogue acquisitions means UMG can deliver strong FCF growth to >€2bn by 2025E, and 11% ahead of consensus. In 2024E, we forecast 12% growth in UMG organic streaming revenues (vs VA cons 11%). On our estimates the group trades on an attractive 4.1% FY25 FCF yield.

Adam Berlin on Publicis Group: We have a Buy rating on Publicis. Our 2025E EPS is 4% ahead of consensus. While a buyback of above €200m would have been an incremental positive, we expect M&A to help Publicis deliver an 8% 2024-26E EPS CAGR. Along with the larger dividend, this implies an annual TSR of 12% even if shares do not continue to re-rate. But we think a further re-rating is still possible with shares trading at 12x.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Communication Services

Polo Tang on Koninklijke KPN NV: we see the company as attractive longer-term but lacking a near-term catalyst. We remain constructive on longer-term fundamentals and see KPN as a well-managed company in an attractive market that should benefit from a step-up in FCF from 2027 when fibre rollout is largely complete. We were previously wary of capex remaining elevated amid fibre overbuild pressure from the likes of ODF/ Delta Fibre but this risk may be receding. However, underlying growth in Wholesale may remain muted with Odido shifting more of its wholesale business to Delta Fiber. Nearterm we see KPN as fairly valued and support from the €200m buyback will fade given it was recently completed.

Ondrej Cabejsek on Tele2 AB: Tele2’s shares have performed well recently, with a new shareholder (link) and a material acceleration in the Swedish unit. We think, however, that consensus still needs to come down by 5-10% on EFCF over the mid-term and that 1Q24 was the peak of service revenue growth given a high base from 2Q4 onwards and competition flagging more efforts to perform better in Sweden. With Telia and Telenor both offering better valuation and more catalysts including consensus upgrades compared to consensus downgrades at Tele2, we think the relative outlook doesn’t favour the Swedish challenger. Based on investor feedback, we consensus may be hopeful that Tele’s new shareholders accelerate some opex/capex efforts. We note, however, that consensus is already pricing in a higher efficiency of the new SEK600m opex program than the previous SEK1bn program, and that we estimate that much of Tele2’s 2024 and 2025 capex is non-negotiable given regulatory requirements to replace Huawei this year and the re-farm 3G to 5G on the back of expiring spectrum next year.

Ondrej Cabejsek on Infrastructure Wireless Italiane: In our recent deep dive (link) we explore Inwit’s exposure the slowing Italian market. We see mid-term positive outcomes for Inwit in case of consolidation that could deleverage and/or increase returns at its core anchors customers, and thus improve their ability to invest. Currently, however, Italy is subject to uncertainty around potential in-market consolidation of telecommunications operators, and pressure on returns at key FWA customers. We believe this will continue to be the case in the near-term, and therefore that sentiment won’t immediately improve around Inwit’s future growth potential. The company offers a solid, c5% dividend yield at a declining leverage (4.7x pro-forma ND/EBITDA) and upside in the case of a minority buyout, but we think the latter may be less likely currently with the aforementioned uncertainty around growth in the market. We also believe that beyond the 2026 guidance period, during which Inwit can simultaneously deleverage and increase shareholder remuneration, FCFE growth is capped by expiring tax benefits that are likely to eat up c60% of EBITDAaL growth between 2026 and 2030, we estimate. We therefore believe that Inwit may eventually have to choose between re-leveraging (albeit well below its leverage policy range) or keeping dividends/buybacks.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Consumer Discretionary

Chris Huang on Bruneloo Cucinelli Spa: We believe 2024 will once again showcase BC's superior fundamentals, highlighted by the overall challenging macro and slowing sector context. We expect the company to deliver +12% organic sales growth in 2024, leading to a +HSD% outperformance vs. the sector, thanks to the brand's absolute luxury positioning which entails strong pricing power and resilient volume growth. This combined with more meaningful operating leverage from 2024 onwards as BC exceeds the €1bn sales mark, drive our +low-teens% 2023-26 EPS CAGR (for more, see Brunello Cucinelli S.p.A “2024 Outlook: Weaving exquisite yet resilient growth”).

Chris Huang on Moncler Spa: In the wider sector context of slowing luxury demand, we believe MONC’s growth will continue to outperform in 2024 (+10% OSG vs. +4% for the sector), driven by space and strong brand momentum. With the group margin still slightly below 2019 levels and the company having prioritised investments so far, we think its margins may only see a slight 20bps contraction y/y given the defensive margin profile. As such, we reiterate our Buy recommendation (for more, see Moncler Spa "2024 Outlook: Earnings resilience despite moderating growth").

Zuzanna Pusz on Hermes: RMS continues to be seen as the luxury sector's most resilient, unique supply-driven equity story, with demand for its leather goods division (\~41% of sales in 2023) exceeding supply. As such we expect 2024 to be a year when the company could consolidate further its position as one of the sector's most defensive stocks amid an industry-wide slow-down with our estimated +14% OSG vs. +4% for peers given more pricing and volume resilience, and very limited EBIT margin pressure (-50bp y/y driven primarily by non-recurrence of hedging gains). RMS remains our top-pick. (for more, see Hermès International SCA "2024 Outlook: Defying gravity").

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Consumer Discretionary

Ben Shelley on Entain Plc: We believe Entain represents good-value access to a structurally growing online market, however, owing to a more muted immediate catalyst pathway, we are more reserved at this juncture. While we think Entain is a collection of mostly attractive businesses, evidenced by strong customer growth and predominantly top 3 market positions, the group faces a mostly negative near-term trajectory, with declining revenues and receding market share across a number of its territories. We believe this leaves investors reluctant to buy into Entain's growth story despite its now undemanding valuation.

Zuzanna Pusz on Burberry Group Plc: We have a Sell rating on the stock. We are cautious on BRBY as we continue to see downside risks to earnings amid the brand’s turnaround, the execution of which further heightened by the uncertain sector backdrop with no signs of brand momentum inflection. Key points in our investment thesis: (1) so far muted reaction of the trade and consumers to the new brand aesthetics and price positioning; (2) concerns around the success of turnaround without the company abandoning its ambitious profitability targets (\~25% EBIT margin) in the ST, to sustainably accelerate its LT topline growth; (3) ultimate decision to be made regarding the brand’s future ie to either meaningfully step up its investments to accelerate LT topline growth or abandon the brand elevation strategy for good, which would also have consequences for its LT margin potential as well as valuation; (4) high relative to peers exposure to the outlet channel on both sales (UBSe \~40%) and EBIT (UBSe \~60%); (5) according to UBS Evidence Lab's Global Luxury Goods Consumer survey, Burberry so far does not see sustainable evidence of brand momentum inflection.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Energy

Henri Patricot on Repsol: We rate Repsol Buy, as we see the company as likely to surprise positively on cashflow generation which should allow to maintain high shareholder returns and transition towards low-carbon faster than peers. We expect the company to continue raising the dividends and maintain buybacks at >€1bn p.a., providing a sectorleading distribution yield of \~14% over 2024-25.

Anna Kishmariya on Orlen: Orlen is the largest energy company in Poland. While the extensive investment plan over the next \~5 years provides for a negative FCF yield on our assumptions, the group has low leverage at this stage and reiterated its commitment to the base dividend which should yield 7.0% for FY24 and 7.2% for FY25. We are cautiously optimistic on the recent management change for the group but consider that for a meaningful re-rating of Orlen shares we would need to see an update on the group’s strategy and projects planned.

Joshua Stone on Eni Spa: We rate the stock Buy. Eni has traded at a 20% EV/DACF average discount to peers over the past five years, reflecting a weaker performance on returns driven by cost overruns and a bias towards a weak Italian downstream market. The stock now trades in line with peers, but we think a premium is now warranted. A new development approach has reduced the risk of project delays in the upstream, natural gas trading margins are supported by restructuring, the transition businesses are gaining scale, and the refining business is being replaced by a higher-returning biofuels offering.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Energy

Henri Patricot on Equinor: We rate Equinor Sell as we see downside risk to European gas prices in the near-term and as a result downside to consensus expectations for this year. We expect shareholder distributions to come down sharply over the next two years and to be already only in line with the sector average next year.

Joshua Stone on OMV AG: For OMV, the petrochemical market remains weak, negotiations on a potential merger in the Middle-East are taking longer than expected, and the stock no longer stands out to us on valuation with a 2024E FCF yield of 12%, only slightly above the peer group. We are Neutral with a PT of €44/sh.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Financials

Nasib Ahmed on Aviva Plc: Buy rated and most preferred life insurance focused name in the UK given high capital return (inc. buybacks) and diversification. Aviva is the only composite insurer in the UK which allows it to grow in markets where the cycle results in the greatest ROC with capital diversification on top.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Financials

Jason Napier on HSBC and BNP: We do not regard either HSBC or BNP as fundamentally expensive given their forecast returns, planned distribution yields and the recent sector pullback triggered in particular by market uncertainty attaching to the announced French elections. We do, however, see both shares as lacking an idiosyncratic catalyst to see them outperform their more focussed sector peers. We rate both Neutral.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Industrials

Sebastian Vogel on Belimo Holdings: We appreciate the structural growth support which the company generally faces. However, with a strong revenue skew towards Europe and non-residential construction (95% of group sales), we expect FY24E group sales growth to fall below the longer term average on the back an expected weak end market. Moreover, the company operates one of the larger FX mismatches in our universe and hence also faces transactional related margin headwinds. We don’t expect this to be reflected in the demanding consensus estimates and the company’s continued high valuation.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Info Tech

Michael Briest on Capgemini: Capgemini’s shares have weakened as commentary from several software companies and smaller IT service peers suggest the demand recovery is being delayed or at the least weakened. Mgt confidently called a trough for growth in Q1, but the pace of recovery remains key.

Michael Briest on Bechtle: Cyclical demand pressures mean Bechtle is likely to be at the lower-end of the 5-10% target growth corridor for 2024. M&A optionality remains.

Michael Briest on Dassault Systemes: Dassault is seeing good traction with 3DExperience in its core, automotive, aerospace and industrial customers. Medidata continues to suffer from the slowdown in the number of clinical trials taking place since the pandemic but a return to y/y growth in H2 is expected.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Info Tech

Harry Blaiklock on Nokia and Ericsson: We believe telco capex spending will continue to be challenged and therefore impact Nokia and Ericsson revenues in 2024 and into 2025. We are also cautious on the structural risk posted by OpenRAN.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Materials

Geoff Haire on Air Liquide: We have a Buy rating on the stock. It has an attractive combination of growing order backlogs (helped by customers de-carbonisation initiatives) and the prospect of volume recovery in the base business.

Gregor Kuglitsch on Heidelberg Materials: We are Neutral on Heidelberg Materials. It should benefit from positive pricing momentum and to a degree declining energy costs which should continue to drive profitability. From here the question is whether earnings momentum will continue in the short-term. Medium-term, the key question is (1) how quickly and at what rate of return the business can decarbonise and (2) how the company will allocate capital given low levels of leverage and high levels of cash generation.

Natasha Brilliant on Vidrala Sa: Vidrala should benefit from wider industry volume trends and we expect a recovery through the 2H after a prolonged period of volume declines/ destocking. Although pricing is likely to moderate this year, we see scope for Vidrala to increase its margins - a testament to its pricing power and position in a consolidated industry landscape.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Materials

Samuel Perry on Lanxess AG: We are Neutral on the stock because 1) EBITDA guidance for this year of 10%-20% growth y/y implies a significant uptick in end market demand, which is not yet visible, and 2) leverage remains high (\~4x ND/EBITDA), which is above sector averages and puts at risk their investment grade credit rating. Furthermore, given the current limited FCF generation of the business the ability to de-lever organically appears challenging.

Priyanka Patel on K+S AG: We are Sell rated on K+S given it is a high cost producer of potash (70% of current production in Germany) and short term we see limited recovery in the potash price, and therefore earnings & FCF, with unsupportive farmer economics. K+S also faces longer-term earnings risk due to increasing potash supply from BHP (2026).

Daniel Major on Boliden: We are Sell rated on BOL as we see earnings pressure from smelters due to lower zinc/copper TCRC’s & mines due to downside risk to zinc prices and we believe this will outweigh tailwinds from copper. Near-term BOL has high capex, -ve FCF and rising net debt and in our view upside risk to 2025 capex may put pressure on capex medium-term.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Real Estate

Zachary Gauge on Segro: The company still has significant reversion to capture from the surge in logistics rents during the COVID period, with £84m in uplifts to come through over the next three years. And although rental growth has slowed, the strong supplydemand dynamics for Urban Logistics are still expected to deliver a robust level of sustainable growth, guided between 3-6%. The company has a sizeable land-bank to develop out and with construction inflation easing and the investment market stabilizing these schemes can now deliver relatively attractive profit margins of between 20-30%. And lastly the company has a small tailwind from their data centre pipeline, largely driven by their sole ownership of the Slough Trading Estate which is already home to the largest cluster of data centres in Europe.

Charles Boissier on Warehouses De Pauw: We have a Buy rating on WDP. Operations continue to benefit from the mega trends in e-commerce, re-shoring and supply chain resilience. More structurally, logistics real estate space per capita in Europe is still a fraction of the more mature US market, hence market rents are still rising in Europe, making Continental European Logistics one of our strongest convictions within European real estate.

Tommaso Operto on Swiss Prime Site: We see SPS as a main beneficiary from reviving real estate transaction volumes through its asset management arm. Despite its operationally streamlined business model, the stock continues to be relatively underowned, and we believe that at the current share discount to NAV, we believe the asset management business is effectively valued at zero.

## Highest-scoring stocks in EU Utilities

Gonzalo Sanchez-Bordona on Enel Spa: We liked the plan presented in the November CMD, with focus on cash generation, cost reduction, capex cuts and potential dividend upside. We see the company delivering higher than expected earnings growth through 2026 and with >8bn lower net capex than in the previous plan. Stock continues trading at an unjustified discount in light of superior dividend, attractive growth and balance sheet derisking. Q1 results surprised positive and give confidence on guidance delivery this year.

Wanda Serwinowska on Engie: In our view, the reasons for Engie's historically low multiples are being addressed systematically, with: (i) volatile commodity-sensitive activities disposed; (ii) opaque energy services activities disposed; (iii) a nuclear exit pathway agreed in Belgium, with liabilities capped at last; (iv) a company-wide cost & simplification programme under way; (v) new multi-year earnings and dividend guidance issued; and (vi) a management focus on consistent delivery. The result should be a more stable and predictable business that no longer deserves the current discount to utility sector multiples, but track records take time to be established and this is likely to be a multi-year process. In the light of the upcoming elections in France, the main concern among investors is a perceived increase in political risk. On our estimates, France accounts for >50% of Engie's EV, however the vast majority is represented by regulated gas networks.

Wanda Serwinowska on E.ON: Our Buy rating is underpinned by: (i) lower risk (75% EBITDA is regulated); (ii) high exposure to accelerating grid spend (e.on has the highest exposure to the electricity distribution grid in Europe across the listed peers with distribution grids accounting for c75% of e.on’s EBITDA) and rising allowed returns; (iii) strong balance sheet relative to other grid operators. The stock trades at a discount to its peers and any move to risk-off or rate-cutting should add further upside, in our view.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in EU Utilities

Gonzalo Sanchez-Bordona on EDP Renovaveis: EDPR is one of the global renewable leaders and we still see a compelling long-term growth story, but short-term issues and potentially lower capital gains after a few stellar years could drag earnings growth. Power price exposure is limited to c15% of sales, and we still see good value creation in new renewable projects, but focus on profitable growth resulted in lower than initially expected additions in 25-26E in the guidance updated in May. Continued challenging earnings momentum on the back of several headwinds and limited upside potential to our PT keeps us Neutral.

Wanda Serwinowska on Fortum OYJ: Fortum is one of the best performing European Utilities over the past 3 months. In our view, the outperformance can be only partly explained by fundamentals (higher power price). Fortum is citied by investors as a play on the theme given the location of its assets and the CO2-free generation. However, we remain cautious on the potential benefits for Fortum, as we note that the company has not signed any long-term PPAs with data centres and Fortum does not see the large premiums for clean power in the Nordics as in the US, given strong supply and limited fossil generation in the region. Fortum trades on at the c30% premium to the sector on the 2026 EV/EBITDA and we expect Fortum EBITDA to decline by 30% over 2023-26 (vs. sector -1%).

## What our Chinese (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/lowest-ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Communication Services

Felix Liu on Bilibili Inc (9626 HK): We like Bilibili for its strong new game performance and market share gain in ad. It is trading below peer average in PS, despite the topline outperformance.

Wei Xiong on Tencent Music Entertainment (TME UN): Despite its valuation premium, we believe TME offers investors with limited macro sensitivity and high earnings visibility. It also enjoys strong market leadership and structural growth in subscriber base and ARPPU, which should support continued solid music revenue growth in the next few years.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Consumer Discretionary

Kenneth Fong on PDD Holdings (PDD UW): PDD is our top pick. Its solid low price mindshare among consumers support PDD as a core beneficiary amid the trend of more rational consumption in China. With PDD trading similarly at 10x P/E as BABA and JD, we see attractive valuation here with PDD’s 65% EPS growth over 2023-25E.

Wei Xiong on Vipshop Holdings (VIPS UN): We believe Vipshop’s near-term growth may see fluctuations given macro and competitive pressure, but the company’s undemanding valuation and aggressive shareholder return programs should offer some downside support to the share price.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Energy

Amily Guo on oil companies - Sinopec and PetroChina (386 HK and 857 HK): We remain bullish on Chin’s Big-3 oil majors because: 1) UBS remains optimistic on oil prices in 2024. The global oil & gas team forecasts the 24-26E brent oil prices average at \$83/80/80/bbl. After the OPEC+’s decision on June 2 to extend production cuts for 3 months, UBS still see a large deficit of 1.2Mb/d in 3Q24 and they expect to support oil prices over the next few weeks, lifting Brent back to the mid to high-\$80s as the deficit materialises; 2) China oil majors’ H-share valuations remain attractive compared to overseas peers, especially US oil companies; 3) High dividends. Sinopec/PetroChina/ CNOOC’s 2023 dividend payouts were 75% (including buyback)/ 50%/44%; and 4) historically, Chinese oil companies have outperformed the market in a tough macro context. We prefer the H shares over the A shares.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Financials

May Yan on Bank Of China (3988 HK): We maintain Buy rating on BOC-H as we think its competitive advantage in offshore business will positively support the group's NIM in a higher-for-longer overseas interest rate environment vs domestic low-rate environment. BOC may also benefit from China's booming outbound investments and trades to replenish fee income and trading gains as it is among the most well-known forex transaction banks in China. We consider BOC-H’s current dividend yield at 6.8% and valuation (0.43x 2024E P/B) still attractive.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Financials

Charles Zhou on New China Life Insurance (1336 HK): We are Neutral on the stock because 1) the company has a relatively high value of new business (VNB) sensitivity to investment return. The interest rate environment in China is still subject to uncertainty, 2) agency productivity is low relative to listed peers, meaning that its growth is more headcount-driven. We highlight the massive recruitment model has become less effective, due to the diminishing demographic dividend, and 3) business quality is not ideal vs peers, as evidenced by its relatively low persistency ratio.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Industrials

Perry Yeung on BOC Aviation (2588 HK): While BOC Aviation (BOCA) has underperformed MSCI AC World by over 30% since December 2022 as investors worry about rising interest rates, we think the share price already factors in a 10-year US treasury yield of more than 5% over the next five years. The market may also have missed the likelihood of impairment write-backs and potential dividend uplift from this, which could give the stock a 2024E dividend yield of 5.5%. Longer term, we think there is scope for BOCA to continue expanding its fleet and earnings (a 10% EPS CAGR in 2024-28E), while tight aircraft supply globally may also be a tailwind.

Robin Xu on Jiangsu Express Co Ltd (177 HK): We have a buy rating on the stock. The legislation of Toll Road Management Ordinance and new tariff scheme for expressway expansion in Jiangsu province provide potential policy support and may help drive further re-rating. In addition, the company is the most defensive among toll road operators in China, as it has never cut its DPS in the past.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in China Industrials

Bruce Mi on Air China: We have a Sell rating on Air China. In our view, the company’s 2024 earnings could miss consensus mainly due to: 1) given China’s mild macro recovery, the downtrading trend and intensifying competition from high-speed rail, Air China’s domestic airfare level could be under pressure in 2024; 2) the company’s international volume recovery could also be dragged by subdued China-US flight volume recovery.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Info Tech

Zoe Xu on Xiaomi Corp (1810 HK): We have a Buy rating on Xiaomi. We like the company for improved scale and profitability of its premium smartphone business. We expect the Xiaomi EV SU7 price reveal at the upcoming launch event to be the next stock price catalysts.

Wendy Zhang on Kingdee International (268 HK): We have a Buy rating on the stock. Kingdee is the second largest Enterprise Resource Planning (ERP) software provider in China and we expect 2023-25 revenue to deliver 17% CAGR benefiting from foreign vendor substitution (eg, Oracle) and enterprise digital transformation. After cloud transition, we expect Kingdee’s operating cash flow to achieve 50% CAGR growing from Rmb600m in 2023E to Rmb1.5bn in 2025, with net profit breakeven by 2025.

## Highest-scoring stocks in China Utilities

Amily Guo on Kunlun Energy Co (135 HK): We like Kunlun as: 1) We expect its retail gas volume growth to maintain 10% in 2024/25, higher than the national average growth (single digit); 2) Although the company’s dollar margin has not expanded in Q124 due to higher contract prices, we expect positive trend in dollar margin with low natural gas procurement prices and price hike for residential end users; 3) Its LNG processing plant business has turned from losses to positive in 2023, and we expect the positive trend to continue with improvements in utilization rate and cost saving; 4) The company raised its dividend payout ratio to 45% in 2025 (from 35%/42% in 2021/22), and we expect is dividend yield at 4.4%/4.7% in 2024/25.

## What our Japanese (fundamental) sector analysts think the highest/ lowest-ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Staples

Faham Baig on Japan Tobacco Inc: We have a Buy rating on the stock. JT is on the front foot in combustibles - helped by peers seeking to transition and its exposure to relatively inelastic emerging markets (c50% of sales) - and an improved offering in heated tobacco should drive an acceleration in EBIT growth, in our view. With the group close to a net cash position, we expect JT to accelerate the dividend and announce a buyback, which would still provide optionality for M&A or MoF stake.

Rei Ihara on Kikkoman Corp: We have a Buy rating on the stock because 1) steady topline of soy sauce in US and EU, 2) low hurdle of international in next 1Q, and 3) historically low valuation.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Health Care

Kasumi Haruta on Chugai Pharmaceutical Co Ltd: Chugai has strong capability for drug discovery especially for antibody, and medium molecule drug discovery could be the next pillar. Profit is likely to be driven by hemophilia drug Hemilbra growth, offsetting rheumatism drug Actemra’s Bio Similar erosion for next few years. Chugai has a highprofile oral GLP-1 agonist orforglipron and is in a leading position globally.

Tomoko Yoshihara on Terumo Corp: We have a Buy rating on the stock because 1) the strong earnings momentum should continue due to the brisk sales in US, cost reduction, and price hike, and 2) plasma source business is on schedule in US and has more potential to expand the client base in the long term.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Japan Health Care

Fumiyoshi Sakai on Sumitomo Pharma Co Ltd: We are Neutral on Sumitomo Pharma. It registered \$2 billion net loss during FY23. F 24 looks another challenging year while the company is in process of cost cutting and redundancy. Top line growth from key products for overactive bladder, endmetriosis/utrine fibrosis and prostate cancer is likely to face tough competition in the USA.

Tomoko Yoshihara on M3 inc: We have a Sell rating on the stock because 1) the sluggish online marketing service sales should not be priced in, and 2) overseas business should remain brisk but not strong enough to offset the weak sales in online marketing service.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Industrials

Harunobu Goroh on Marubeni Corp: We expect the profit growth trend to be maintained with capital allocation centered on strengthening existing business areas. Also, the company’s financial condition has already been improving, and its ability to increase shareholder payouts by reallocating free cash flow also should support the stock price.

Harunobu Goroh on Itochu Corp: We expect the management resource allocation centers on the middle risk/return-type businesses, while the time axis for the start of contribution to returns is relatively short. High yields in investment returns are expected, which is an advantage in terms of visibility.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Japan Materials

Harunobu Goroh on Mitsui Mining & Smelting: We believe that ROE improvement and Cost of equity reduction drives PBR multiple higher. The copper foil earnings contribution set to grow with Ai server related demand, and recovery of auto part related segment will be another positive factor.

Harunobu Goroh on Kobe Steel Ltd: We believe that the three business areas would be well-balanced and make positive contribution respectively for improved PBR multiple. The materials is set to recover by securing appropriate margin, the machinery got record high level of orders in FY23, electric power generates stable cash flow.

## What our Australian (fundamental) sector analysts think about the highest/lowest ranked stocks

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Consumer Discretionary

Shaun Cousins on Super Retail Group: SUL share price has fallen in recent months and this creates a more attractive risk reward with (1) SuperCheapAuto (49% of UBSe FY24 EBIT) a resilient base; (2) sales gth drivers across stores, online, club and loyalty; (3) EBIT margin headwinds due to rising CODB yet supply chain optimisation a medium term tailwind; and (4) Capital mgmt. expected at FY24E result to get closer to its capital mgmt. framework.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Consumer Discretionary

Shaun Cousins on Domino's Pizza Enterprises: DMP execution has been mixed over recent years following a period where COVID was an accelerant (delivery skew, peer channels closed in Japan). Recent acquisitions have created a combination of markets where DMP has been unable to execute well, with management looking somewhat stretched. Slower store gth and slower EBIT margin expansion drove lower UBSe EPS estimates and makes share price outperformance unlikely, yet arguably provides DMP a more sound footing for future gth. Despite these challenges, we believe share price performance (since 3-Jul-23/3-Jan-24 DMP -14.0%/-29.1% vs ASX200 +5.0%/+1.1%) and improving SSSg (ANZ strengthening, Germany performing & Japan positive albeit early) make the risk reward more balanced; Neutral.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Staples

Shaun Cousins on Coles Group Ltd: Performance in the core Supermarkets division is improving (LFL sales gth to start CY24E, gross margin tailwind as theft headwind reverses, some early cost savings from Witron automated DC) yet headwinds exist across Liquor & Other divisions. The COL P/E multiple remains at a discount to WOW, albeit less than the average since Nov-18. Risk/reward balanced yet preferred vs WOW (both Neutral rated).

Apoorv Sehgal on Graincorp Ltd: Buy based on through-the-cycle earnings and scope for capital returns. We expect a likely working capital unwind over FY24E (as crop conditions normalise in dryer conditions) to support a net core cash position expanding from \$200m at 1H23 to \$460m by FY24E, or 30% of mcap, before likely \~\$104m of proceeds from sale of its 8.5% UMG stake. We expect GNC to return a portion of this cash to shareholders. Stock trading on \~5x through-the-cycle EBITDA looks relatively cheap.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Staples

Shaun Cousins on Woolworths Group Ltd: We are Neutral on WOW. Its 1H24E was preguided yet the shape of the core Aust Food division and concerns about sales gth with CODB pressures to continue, as well as ongoing questions on the outlook for NZ Food & Big W, weighed on investor sentiment and drive UBSe EBIT lower. Further, the Aust Food industry is facing multiple regulatory reviews (Senate enquiry, the scheduled food and grocery code of conduct review, pricing & competition review by the ACCC), while the retirement of CEO Brad Banducci, who is well regarded, was unexpected, or at least earlier than expected.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Health Care

Laura Sutcliffe on Telix Pharmaceuticals Ltd: Top pick in Australian healthcare – high growth, CF positive biotech with imminent pipeline developments.

Laura Sutcliffe on CSL Ltd: We see upside risk to 2-3yr earnings estimates as delivery on components of gross margin recovery materialises.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Industrials

Andre Fromyhr on Brambles Ltd: We expect growing confidence in Brambles' FCF recovery to drive re-rating of the multiple. BXB is trading well below ASX industrials, which we think is too low and suggests disbelief in the sustainability of earnings and FCF. We think the market is currently too focused on top line / cyclical momentum but more attention should be placed on margin stability and FCF improvement.

Nathan Reilly on ALS Ltd: Recently delivered its FY24 result, with EPS growth outlook is improving (UBSe +4%/10% growth in FY25/26E), but appears priced in. We see the turnaround of Nuvisan, integration of recent acquisitions and a recovery in exploration activity as key catalysts for the stock into FY25. We look to the upcoming AGM in July for formal earnings guidance and further colour on key end market outlooks. FY24 result: Initial FY25 outlook commentary consistent with UBSe.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Industrials

Nathan Reilly on Monadelphous Group: In our view, solid order book momentum and increasing resource sector capex commitments should support Monadelphous' earnings in FY24-25. As such, we expect the company to deliver revenue growth of 11% in FY24 (in line with guidance), and we forecast FY24/25 EPS growth of +17%/+8%. We highlight that skilled labour shortages and supply chain constraints are still a challenge for Monadelphous, which the company continues to actively manage. We look to the upcoming FY24 result in August as the next key catalyst for the stock. Monadelphous Group "1H24 result right in line with market expectations"

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Materials

Lee Power on James Hardie Industries: We have a Buy rating on JHX because 1) the company is a lead manufacturer of fibre cement in the US which is a growth and market penetration story in the US, 2) has exposure to strong US housing structural themes with significant undersupply of housing providing a higher earnings floor for a cyclical, 3) trades in-line with US new home builders but >55% of North American revenue is exposed to the more resilient Repair and Remodel (R&R) market, and 4) its EBIT margins recently benefitting from structural lift due to new manufacturing processes.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Materials

Levi Spry on Pilbara Minerals: We have a Sell rating on PLS. It is still holding up too well versus our view on lithium markets being oversupplied in the short run.

## Highest-scoring stocks in Australia Real Estate

Tom Bodor on Mirvac Group: While we see no OEPS growth into FY25 and some risk to FY24 guidance (if 55 Pitt St slips past 30-Jun) recent MGR underperformance is notable. MGR has been the worst performing REIT over the last month (-9%, A0REIT 200 +2%) and the medium term OEPS growth to FY28 of 6% is ahead of the sector, despite a P/e of 13c (vs the sector at 21x). We see MGR as being a good resi recovery exposure, though delays to interest rate cuts could defer this benefit.

Tom Bodor on GPT Group: We see value in GPT's unique blend of niche retail and quality logistics assets (somewhat weighed by current office headwinds) with a new strategy from the CEO transition a clear catalyst for unlocking value in the portfolio.

## Lowest-scoring stocks in Australia Real Estate

Tom Bodor on Lifestyle Communities: LIC is a quality business operating in a favourable sub sector but has been exposed to the weak Victorian residential market, where we see limited evidence of a recovery. Investors are questioning the previous approach of utilising high gearing to fund the scaling of development activities.

Cody Shield on Charter Hall Retail Reit: We are Neutral on the stock. CQR's portfolio income growth continues to benefit from increased exposure to long WALE convenience retail assets with CPI linked income and triple net leases; however, this isn't enough to offset debt cost headwinds in our view.

## All regions in one chart

Figure 151: Price momentum has started to fade in Europe & Canada, and even turned negative again in China. Most favoured sectors remain Financials, Tech & Energy. Worth noting that Utilities now exhibit the wort momentum, after losing a lot of traction in the last 4/5 weeks
![](images/03f54102322313e3e47c57c9b66c4eb1daa3f4dae868c30c7a29c0b8b33f129b.jpg)

![](images/53cc54fe5b7435fd2a71fc0949e41ec209fcd882381ee1171353889912ac49d9.jpg)

![](images/74b2ce6bf663d0be2be24d5a5fd15a0e3077b962b55c81d5df81b88f0af41253.jpg)

![](images/432a8f13b46cef9d036aad7e0ebd0c1dca2b3dbf579a174516ae8aad5fcfe2cb.jpg)

![](images/7198a0d2d5d7aef281b536d808d344277e8ba350f4b2fb1994756f7f3240c8be.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, MSCI, UBS

![](images/cec60225e8fb73d3445c01f5cbf794319f0dec1b54e622dba820052638b9686e.jpg)

![](images/27f3086becc760c47fe1139b5e2e26441c2889d4d30d2352ab6cf88cce749b0a.jpg)

Figure 152: Not much change in Positioning vs. last month, it remains very supportive in China, while being more balanced in other regions. In US and Europe, Real estate is still the sector to overweight based on positioning, while in other regions, the top pick is Info Tech. Staples remains the sector to stay away from, as investors are still building shorts without any sign of crowding yet

![](images/1aaaca69fa8ab30324c4853968613ff97ad61e75d119a3a28a2a464f5048f485.jpg)

![](images/19030fc45ed664fab0283360c8ad4e9d452993c1747bb03de2781d52a7428158.jpg)

![](images/776b7cdfcfaa1c8b400c6fc21936cc82ad118225ef67a649a98fab43ea3bcab6.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, MSCI, UBS

![](images/4b1c7311aed5c4cc50f518741ca49dc0edf52374b5e0af63aafe2279ea520abd.jpg)

Figure 153: Earnings expectations and momentum continue to favour Real Estate (ex China). Materials stocks seem to face some (recent & strong) challenges, as EPS momentum has seriously weakened, and turned negative this month. Defensive sectors are still struggling to move up in the ranking, despite being in the late stage of the business cycle
![](images/042ed521fbe8095950639d67ec767fefe3fd3cc3b7003a69db728c71b067d760.jpg)

![](images/fb4a386ee47641f134eede17d7e03d9301767957dd3d669a4becf44e339dbae9.jpg)

![](images/fc614117abffc7a15be767d740b41b411bb16ed127605fb080ee2add6216ce0d.jpg)

![](images/696b3a8d00a656593fd74ba3eae802d91deae6f65d5229ed0e42ad468ff7dffc.jpg)

![](images/efd562195449b0b561ad04ce491391f0563f42bb57c731829ee1a615711ed888.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, MSCI, UBS

![](images/40337391dfab78d56204c77feb2fb086d02f9f42116ac91a9148d756176621c0.jpg)

Figure 154: OECD G20 Composite Leading index latest print and revision suggest we will soon switch from an expansion to a slowdown regime. This should be less bullish cyclicals, and our regime model is getting prepared for a sector rotation. Energy has been cut first. Are Industrials next?
![](images/2e9b0fd4320769f931b87c88be538daa69e6a686ae78a4316a24ccd5635e4135.jpg)

![](images/c7dd5bf752194ec58ad38ce74622dab6b58492a72b820e3721a4aa70d3a4a6e3.jpg)

![](images/add7ae2b397e363eb6a792aba530203b89067379b07b7f1f80e729df2944c547.jpg)

![](images/cc518d91d3279b81f2937214fd026fec8530650eb723bd95104a6afb6f53f6be.jpg)

![](images/63b36012b5962733566c5a15a187c232fdc8ddc8a7b93e77591eb520e4416139.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, MSCI, UBS

![](images/c4e2168c5ab9690543a0f10893402deed4c052e25bc918fc2b5da0634e85d6bd.jpg)

Figure 155: On valuation, Financials & Utilities remain the cheapest sectors in our adjusted 12mth fwd PE model, when Tech & Health Care remain the most expensive. Value is a long term game. Since the last update, relative valuations have (unsurprisingly) worsened in Tech
![](images/20b99e7782532300d10ef1cacb0e70223f8d345807010401ea31dd9f37aa3869.jpg)

![](images/1ace885753e7fe32e5d43f30e75e0fb3ebd1bc35fae4b14625bd128a2f1c8321.jpg)

![](images/ed764f612e26e5fd01bae6c685987aae5cf20ddd09aea4e24c960347c580aa30.jpg)

![](images/41fe18ea5804d029f4f1054e45c3385fae0ba76dc647054319019f7293b570e5.jpg)

![](images/7b4583faa0a33ecdc75f7c294561303b8b2724edb510d19ec8d77a65894ed86d.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, MSCI, UBS

![](images/f6e05a15307ebc8af9c12a8a8178bf17e3ee152fbf36e5dca6161a0a895c43e6.jpg)

Figure 156: After Health Care in May, it is Staples which experienced a significant improvement in its Sector PMI new orders in June. Coming back to Health Care PMI, it continues its exponential rise, which our model wants to fade, given the high probability of a short term mean reversion
![](images/c654ce8ba08fa4b208e6e3ad6559142f06e63241502d13fb1c8063ee1b360389.jpg)

![](images/b3f51a3d7958f5360994941f8d9c6c5de50340bebec484ab537facfcaae01a9d.jpg)

![](images/68fb9e69c4d4436c31ff33641db6b3b2f6d27da672a1ea8917520807d8a9d6f3.jpg)

![](images/c51c58b41edde7f52dccb1f637d6c95e90634fbde6ee1bb0205a2052cbd275c8.jpg)

![](images/a93c7c9189d2c08c53d37f377fa3a2f510875d27b5495e42b907d0d15b933e6f.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, MSCI, UBS

![](images/8e3341ac906363c2a76e3d8a4725773b355c9ba883ef72743c5b8286384d4cfb.jpg)

Figure 157: Overall, the model is still displaying a risk-on stance but has reduced its level of conviction by 20% (the average signal is at +0.39 vs. +0.51 last month). Cyclical sectors - Industrials, Financials, Energy - are still favoured, when defensive sectors - Health Care and Staples - still appear less attractive. Real Estate & Tech are moving up in the rankings (vs. the previous update), while Energy & Utilities are moving down
![](images/4345b3589cf08909068810d260cad2e01518bcde8b47473948b2bae9c5e46f00.jpg)

![](images/d6d01dc80b604c577229bb535223e28c94d5442277ebefd6bf77d9f3afe76a2b.jpg)

![](images/b61183915f8b162262b76df898092579962d615c49007327651a48d3d921173c.jpg)

![](images/8fc9b8138c5210fc88b8dc03137d6fbca236c254cafda6d008c97fc0c770a216.jpg)

![](images/0c63b8096760d6107ae70c840d9f7615f8995dbb5ad336b0dd627295dc5ee184.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, MSCI, UBS

## Valuation Method and Risk Statement

Risks of multi-asset investing include but are not limited to market risk, credit risk, interest rate risk, and foreign exchange risk. Correlations of returns among different asset classes may deviate from historical patterns. Geopolitical events and policy shocks pose risks that can reduce asset returns. Valuations may be adversely affected during times of high market volatility, thin liquidity, and economic dislocation.

KEY DEFINITIONS: Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR) Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Short-Term Ratings reflect the expected near-term (up to three months) performance of the stock and do not reflect any change in the fundamental view or investment case. Equity Price Targets have an investment horizon of 12 months.

EXCEPTIONS AND SPECIAL CASES: UK and European Investment Fund ratings and definitions are: Buy: Positive on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Neutral: Neutral on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Sell: Negative on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount. Core Banding Exceptions (CBE): Exceptions to the standard +/-6% bands may be granted by the Investment Review Committee (IRC). Factors considered by the IRC include the stock's volatility and the credit spread of the respective company's debt. As a result, stocks deemed to be very high or low risk may be subject to higher or lower bands as they relate to the rating. When such exceptions apply, they will be identified in the Company Disclosures table in the relevant research piece.
